Your review record

Review, save, and rescan

Use Record your review beside a finding, acceptance check, or coverage gap. Keep the observed result and your rationale together. Justifications are user decisions, never automatic passes.

  1. Review findings and acceptance checks; add an owner, reason and supporting reference.
  2. Choose Download reviewed HTML below. Use this button instead of the browser’s Save As command so form edits and import metadata are preserved.
  3. Scan the current source/image again using the saved file: invarune ./repository --review-report ./invarune-reviewed.html --output ./new-report.

Rescanning checks the current evidence before reusing decisions. Changed or out-of-scope records remain visible for review; a missing detection is not proof of remediation. Runtime and human validation notes do not waive coverage gaps.

Action plan

Priority actions and defense layers

Back to top

Each group connects observed evidence to a practical first action, an accountable team, and additional safeguards. These layers are proposed, not verified; they do not automatically reduce finding severity or remove the need to fix the underlying condition.

P1 high open AI001

Dynamic Python code execution

2 occurrence(s) · Suggested owner: Agent/tool developer

Why this matters

If external or generated content reaches this expression, it could execute Python with the process identity and access its files, credentials, and network.

Immediate action

Trace the input to eval/exec and replace it with fixed operations; if generated execution is essential, isolate it before accepting untrusted input.

Evidence context: source

Source evidence: deployment reachability and active use have not been established.

Affected locations (2)
  • python/packages/autogen-core/src/autogen_core/tools/_function_tool.py:159
  • python/packages/autogen-core/src/autogen_core/tools/_function_tool.py:171

Evidence: 3267af939c6985b894806d14, 34f362cd01faaef85332b14e

Related controls: EXEC-02

Additional defenses to verify: Enforce action policy outside the model • Isolate tool execution

Additional layers that can lower risk (2)

Enforce action policy outside the model

How it lowers risk: Have an execution service allow only named operations, permitted argument values, and caller-owned target resources before invoking a tool.

Evidence to verify: Exercise disallowed operations, additional arguments, cross-tenant identifiers, and direct calls that bypass the agent planner.

Residual limitation: Authorized operations can still be harmful when business constraints are incomplete; prompt instructions cannot enforce this boundary.

Verification status: proposed_not_verified

Related controls: AGT-01, AUTH-02, MCP-02

Guidance sources
  • MCP-TOOLS / MCP: Tools
  • OWASP-AGENT-CS / AI Agent Security Cheat Sheet

Isolate tool execution

How it lowers risk: Run the risky tool in a separate, disposable identity with no host mounts, ambient credentials, or unnecessary outbound access.

Evidence to verify: In a test environment, attempt reads outside the workspace, forbidden network connections, and resource exhaustion; retain policy and denial evidence.

Residual limitation: Isolation limits reachable assets; it does not make injected code trustworthy or rule out a runtime escape.

Verification status: proposed_not_verified

Related controls: EXEC-02, SUP-05, OPS-02

Guidance sources
  • JOINT-DEPLOY / Deploying AI Systems Securely: Best Practices for Deploying Secure and Resilient AI Systems
Guidance sources (5)
Guidance sources

P1 high open AI005

Executable deserialization requires trusted inputs

2 occurrence(s) · Suggested owner: Application/model pipeline owner

Why this matters

If an attacker can supply or replace the serialized input, loading it may execute code even before the application examines the returned object.

Immediate action

Establish who can produce and replace the input; migrate to a nonexecutable data format or restrict verified legacy artifacts to an isolated conversion path.

Evidence context: source

Source evidence: deployment reachability and active use have not been established.

Affected locations (2)
  • python/packages/autogen-ext/src/autogen_ext/experimental/task_centric_memory/_memory_bank.py:82
  • python/packages/autogen-ext/src/autogen_ext/experimental/task_centric_memory/_string_similarity_map.py:48

Evidence: e7b59756ebdbaf6453e8bf68, 49ef15a1e06ab2906b0ae813

Related controls: EXEC-06

Additional defenses to verify: Verify artifact identity before use • Separate artifact conversion from production

Additional layers that can lower risk (2)

Verify artifact identity before use

How it lowers risk: Require reviewed artifact provenance plus a digest or signature checked against an independently trusted identity or manifest.

Evidence to verify: Substitute bytes, producer identity, or verification metadata in a controlled test and confirm the consumer rejects the artifact.

Residual limitation: Authenticity establishes the producer and bytes; an approved producer can still ship vulnerable or malicious content.

Verification status: proposed_not_verified

Related controls: SUP-03, DATA-04

Guidance sources
  • OPENSSF-MODEL-SIGNING / Launch of Model Signing v1.0
  • SLSA-12 / Supply-chain Levels for Software Artifacts specification

Separate artifact conversion from production

How it lowers risk: Perform necessary conversion of legacy serialized artifacts in a disposable environment without production data or credentials.

Evidence to verify: Demonstrate that a conversion job cannot reach production services and only approved output formats are promoted.

Residual limitation: Conversion output still needs integrity, format, and downstream behavior review; isolation is not proof of benign content.

Verification status: proposed_not_verified

Related controls: EXEC-06, SUP-06

Guidance sources
  • JOINT-DEPLOY / Deploying AI Systems Securely: Best Practices for Deploying Secure and Resilient AI Systems
  • TECH-PYTORCH-SERIAL / PyTorch Serialization semantics
Guidance sources (6)
Guidance sources

P2 medium open AI009

Application debug mode enabled

1 occurrence(s) · Suggested owner: Application and platform owner

Why this matters

If debug behavior is active on a reachable deployment, errors or a framework debugger may disclose internals or permit additional actions.

Immediate action

Verify the effective deployed setting, disable debug behavior, and confirm representative errors do not expose a debugger or sensitive details.

Evidence context: source

Source evidence: deployment reachability and active use have not been established.

Affected locations (1)
  • python/packages/autogen-studio/autogenstudio/web/app.py:60

Evidence: 8f8e715e8f65d28379ca9bd4

Related controls: DATA-03

Additional defenses to verify: Separate operational diagnostics from client errors • Enforce reviewed deployment defaults

Additional layers that can lower risk (2)

Separate operational diagnostics from client errors

How it lowers risk: Return minimal errors to clients and retain necessary diagnostic detail only in protected, redacted server-side records.

Evidence to verify: Trigger representative failures and confirm public responses exclude stack traces, credentials, paths, and debug consoles.

Residual limitation: Redaction can miss unexpected formats, and authorized log readers may still see sensitive operational context.

Verification status: proposed_not_verified

Related controls: DATA-03, OPS-01

Guidance sources
  • CISA-SECURE-BY-DESIGN / Shifting the Balance of Cybersecurity Risk: Principles and Approaches for Secure by Design Software
  • NCSC-SECURE-AI / Guidelines for secure AI system development

Enforce reviewed deployment defaults

How it lowers risk: Gate releases on approved runtime settings and reject deployments that restore the unsafe option through overrides.

Evidence to verify: Evaluate the rendered deployment and running workload, then attempt a rollout containing the forbidden override.

Residual limitation: A source configuration or successful build does not establish the effective production runtime policy.

Verification status: proposed_not_verified

Related controls: SUP-04, SUP-05, GOV-05

Guidance sources
  • NCSC-SECURE-AI / Guidelines for secure AI system development
  • NIST-SSDF / Secure Software Development Framework (SSDF) Version 1.1

P2 medium open AI033

Potential secret included in logging call

2 occurrence(s) · Suggested owner: Application/observability owner

Why this matters

If this value is a real secret, log readers, exporters, or retained telemetry copies may gain its permissions beyond the original application boundary.

Immediate action

Verify the logged value safely, remove it before formatting/export, and rotate it if real exposure is confirmed.

Evidence context: source

Source evidence: deployment reachability and active use have not been established.

Affected locations (2)
  • python/packages/autogen-studio/autogenstudio/web/auth/manager.py:98
  • python/packages/autogen-studio/autogenstudio/web/auth/manager.py:101

Evidence: 4c41f364f09aeb4440e11ab1, c895143b34bac6dbd8bd111d

Related controls: DATA-03

Additional defenses to verify: Minimize and protect retained telemetry • Limit credentials available to the workload

Additional layers that can lower risk (2)

Minimize and protect retained telemetry

How it lowers risk: Collect only necessary nonsecret events, redact before export, and limit access, retention, and downstream replication.

Evidence to verify: Inject recognizable synthetic secret values and trace their absence through application, proxy, tracing, and archived logs.

Residual limitation: Redaction tests cover selected formats; already exported copies and authorized readers remain part of the exposure assessment.

Verification status: proposed_not_verified

Related controls: DATA-03, DATA-05, OPS-01

Guidance sources
  • NCSC-SECURE-AI / Guidelines for secure AI system development
  • NIST-GENAI / Artificial Intelligence Risk Management Framework: Generative Artificial Intelligence Profile

Limit credentials available to the workload

How it lowers risk: Give this component a distinct identity and only the downstream scopes and lifetime needed for its approved operations.

Evidence to verify: Use its runtime identity to attempt forbidden service operations, then revoke it and confirm subsequent access fails.

Residual limitation: A compromised component can still use its allowed permissions until credentials expire or revocation takes effect.

Verification status: proposed_not_verified

Related controls: AUTH-05, AUTH-09

Guidance sources
  • JOINT-AGENTIC / Careful adoption of agentic AI services
Guidance sources (5)
Guidance sources

P3 low open AI024

Container image is not digest pinned

4 occurrence(s) · Suggested owner: Container/release owner

Why this matters

If a mutable image tag changes, a rebuild or deployment may consume unreviewed bytes; the scan does not establish that the current image is vulnerable.

Immediate action

Identify the approved image digest and enforce it in the effective build or deployment while preserving a reviewed update process.

Evidence context: source

Source evidence: deployment reachability and active use have not been established.

Keep pinned artifacts maintained

How it lowers risk: Review known-vulnerability and maintenance evidence for the actual resolved artifacts and apply updates through a repeatable release process.

Evidence to verify: Demonstrate a dependency update, relevant regression checks, and deployment or rollback of the resulting approved artifact.

Residual limitation: This source/image scan does not provide a current CVE verdict, and a vulnerability database cannot identify every unknown flaw.

highopenAI001

Dynamic Python code execution

python/packages/autogen-core/src/autogen_core/tools/_function_tool.py:159–159

Finding ID: 3267af939c6985b894806d14
Confidence: medium · Category: tool_execution

eval or exec receives a nonliteral expression. If agent, user, or tool content can reach it, it can execute arbitrary Python. Static analysis does not establish the full data flow.

Observed evidence

                exec(import_code, exec_globals)

Remediation: Replace dynamic execution with an allowlisted operation dispatcher; use ast.literal_eval only for bounded literal parsing. Isolate unavoidable execution with no ambient credentials and strict resource limits.

Fix plan and agent/MCP relevance

Replace dynamic Python evaluation with a bounded operation interface.

Why this matters for agents/MCP: An agent can convert a prompt or MCP tool result into an eval/exec argument. If that value reaches this call, model influence becomes Python execution with the tool process identity.

Inspect the reported source location and its callers, change the implementation or effective configuration, then rebuild and rescan. The scan does not establish that this code is on an active agent or MCP request path.

Confirm applicability

  • Trace the expression from its producer to this exact call; a nonliteral expression alone does not establish external control or an active agent path.
  • Trusted fixed startup code and deliberately isolated code-execution services need different treatment; document which boundary applies.
  1. Remove the interpreter boundary

    Replace eval/exec with a dictionary of fixed callable operations and validate a small JSON argument schema before selecting a callable. Do not permit callers to name arbitrary modules, attributes or Python expressions.

    Verify: Send unsupported operation names and extra arguments through the same tool entry point; verify rejection before any callable executes.

  2. Choose a bounded data parser

    For data, prefer JSON plus explicit type, length and nesting limits. Use ast.literal_eval only when Python literals are genuinely required, with independent input and resource bounds; it is not a general untrusted-input sandbox.

    Verify: Exercise malformed, oversized and deeply nested values in an isolated test and confirm bounded failure without evaluating names or calls.

  3. Isolate required generated execution

    If code execution is the product feature, route it to a disposable worker with a separate identity, scoped workspace, no ambient credentials, bounded CPU/memory/time and denied-by-default egress.

    Verify: Attempt a forbidden workspace read, network request and long-running task in a test worker; retain denials and verify cleanup.

Remaining validation

  • A permitted operation may still violate tenant or business policy; authorize target resources outside the model.
  • Rescanning can confirm removal of a recognized expression, not sandbox strength or absence of alternate execution paths.

Related controls: EXEC-02

Fix guidance sources
  • REM-PY-AST / Python ast.literal_eval security and resource limits
  • OWASP-AGENT-CS / AI Agent Security Cheat Sheet
  • JOINT-DEPLOY / Deploying AI Systems Securely: Best Practices for Deploying Secure and Resilient AI Systems

Weakness mappings: CWE-95

Finding references
  • https://docs.python.org/3/library/security_warnings.html
  • https://genai.owasp.org/resource/owasp-genai-llm-top-10-2026/
Record your review unreviewed

highopenAI005

Executable deserialization requires trusted inputs

python/packages/autogen-ext/src/autogen_ext/experimental/task_centric_memory/_memory_bank.py:82–82

Finding ID: e7b59756ebdbaf6453e8bf68
Confidence: medium · Category: deserialization

Pickle-compatible deserialization can execute code. This finding identifies a dangerous trust boundary, not proof that the serialized input is attacker controlled.

Observed evidence

                self.uid_memo_dict = pickle.load(f)

Remediation: Use a nonexecutable serialization format with schema validation. If compatibility requires pickle, accept only authenticated artifacts from a strictly controlled producer.

Fix plan and agent/MCP relevance

Replace executable object deserialization or tightly authenticate its producer.

Why this matters for agents/MCP: A cached agent state, plugin artifact or MCP-uploaded file may reach pickle-compatible loading. Deserialization can execute producer-selected code before downstream data validation.

Inspect the reported source location and its callers, change the implementation or effective configuration, then rebuild and rescan. The scan does not establish that this code is on an active agent or MCP request path.

Confirm applicability

  • Identify the producer and every write path to the artifact, including shared caches, object stores and task workspaces.
  • The finding identifies a dangerous loader; it does not show that its input is attacker controlled.
  1. Migrate to data-only serialization

    Replace pickle, dill, joblib or equivalent object loading with JSON or another nonexecutable format plus a versioned schema. Reconstruct only explicitly supported application types.

    Verify: Roundtrip required benign state and reject unexpected type markers, fields and oversized collections without importing producer-selected code.

  2. Control unavoidable legacy artifacts

    If compatibility requires executable deserialization, authenticate the exact bytes against a producer/key or registry digest established independently of the artifact location. Restrict cache and artifact writers.

    Verify: Alter one byte and change the producer identity in test artifacts; verify rejection before the deserializer is called.

  3. Quarantine conversion

    Convert legacy artifacts in a disposable credential-free worker with resource and network limits, then export validated data to the main service.

    Verify: Verify conversion cannot read main-service secrets or contact forbidden destinations and that failed conversions publish no partial trusted state.

Remaining validation

  • A valid signature identifies a producer; it does not make a compromised or malicious producer safe.
  • Schema validation after pickle loading is too late to prevent execution during loading.

Related controls: EXEC-06

Fix guidance sources
  • TECH-PYTHON-SECURITY / Python Security Considerations
  • JOINT-DEPLOY / Deploying AI Systems Securely: Best Practices for Deploying Secure and Resilient AI Systems

Weakness mappings: CWE-502

Finding references
  • https://docs.python.org/3/library/security_warnings.html
Record your review unreviewed

    If compatibility requires executable deserialization, authenticate the exact bytes against a producer/key or registry digest established independently of the artifact location. Restrict cache and artifact writers.

    Verify: Alter one byte and change the producer identity in test artifacts; verify rejection before the deserializer is called.

  3. Quarantine conversion

    Convert legacy artifacts in a disposable credential-free worker with resource and network limits, then export validated data to the main service.

    Verify: Verify conversion cannot read main-service secrets or contact forbidden destinations and that failed conversions publish no partial trusted state.

Remaining validation

  • A valid signature identifies a producer; it does not make a compromised or malicious producer safe.
  • Schema validation after pickle loading is too late to prevent execution during loading.

Related controls: EXEC-06

Fix guidance sources
  • TECH-PYTHON-SECURITY / Python Security Considerations
  • JOINT-DEPLOY / Deploying AI Systems Securely: Best Practices for Deploying Secure and Resilient AI Systems

Weakness mappings: CWE-502

Finding references
  • https://docs.python.org/3/library/security_warnings.html
Record your review unreviewed

Justified or disabled requires a reason and reviewer. Notes and runtime/human validation requests do not remove an open finding or establish a pass.

mediumopenAI009

Application debug mode enabled

python/packages/autogen-studio/autogenstudio/web/app.py:60–60

Finding ID: 8f8e715e8f65d28379ca9bd4
Confidence: high · Category: configuration

Debug mode is explicitly enabled in server code or configuration. Depending on the framework and deployment, error details or a debugger may become externally accessible.

Observed evidence

app = FastAPI(lifespan=lifespan, debug=True)

Remediation: Disable debug mode in deployed services and return sanitized errors while retaining protected server-side diagnostics.

Fix plan and agent/MCP relevance

Disable deployed debug behavior without losing protected diagnostics.

Why this matters for agents/MCP: An agent API or MCP server may handle secrets, prompts and privileged tools. A reachable development debugger or verbose exception page can reveal that state and, in some frameworks, permit code execution.

Inspect the reported source location and its callers, change the implementation or effective configuration, then rebuild and rescan. The scan does not establish that this code is on an active agent or MCP request path.

Confirm applicability

  • Resolve the framework and final deployment settings; debug behavior varies and a test-only setting is not evidence of public exposure.
  • A framework debug flag, log level and interactive debugger are different controls and may require separate settings.
  1. Disable runtime debug and debugger switches

    Set the production server/framework debug option to false, remove development-server launch flags, and use the supported production server configuration.

    Verify: Start the deployed configuration in staging and trigger a controlled exception; verify there is no interactive debugger or detailed client traceback.

  2. Separate user errors from protected logs

    Return a minimal error identifier and safe message. Keep detailed server diagnostics in access-controlled logs with credential/prompt redaction.

    Verify: Use a synthetic secret marker in a failing request and confirm it is absent from the HTTP/MCP error and unauthorized log views.

  3. Prevent accidental re-enablement

    Remove debug overrides from environment files, image defaults and orchestration manifests; require an explicit separate development profile.

    Verify: Inspect effective deployment settings and validate the production startup fails or alerts if the development profile is selected.

Remaining validation

  • Disabling debug does not remove injection defects or secrets already emitted to clients and logs.

Related controls: DATA-03

Fix guidance sources
  • REM-FLASK-DEBUG / Flask: debugging application errors
  • CISA-SECURE-BY-DESIGN / Shifting the Balance of Cybersecurity Risk: Principles and Approaches for Secure by Design Software

Weakness mappings: CWE-489

Finding references
  • https://www.cisa.gov/resources-tools/resources/secure-by-design

mediumopenAI033

Potential secret included in logging call

python/packages/autogen-studio/autogenstudio/web/auth/manager.py:98–98

Finding ID: 4c41f364f09aeb4440e11ab1
Confidence: medium · Category: data_protection

A logging call directly references a credential-named variable or field. Log processors and retention can broaden access to secrets.

Observed evidence

            logger.warning(f"Expired token received: {token[:10]}...")

Remediation: Log nonsecret identifiers and outcomes; redact credentials before formatting and restrict log access and retention.

Fix plan and agent/MCP relevance

Remove credential values from logging before formatting or export.

Why this matters for agents/MCP: Agent traces and MCP logs commonly cross observability vendors, support systems and retention tiers. Logging a credential widens who can use the underlying model or tool authority beyond its intended worker.

Inspect the reported source location and its callers, change the implementation or effective configuration, then rebuild and rescan. The scan does not establish that this code is on an active agent or MCP request path.

Confirm applicability

  • A credential-named variable may hold a nonsecret identifier; classify the value and its logging path without disclosing it.
  • Check structured logs, exception serialization, tracing spans and downstream collectors as well as the source logging call.
  1. Log identifiers and outcomes instead

    Replace logged secret variables with request IDs, credential fingerprints suitable for the system, or nonsecret status. Redact at the source before string formatting and serialization.

    Verify: Run success/error paths with a synthetic credential marker and confirm it is absent from local logs, structured fields and exported traces.

  2. Contain confirmed historical exposure

    Rotate actual exposed credentials and locate affected log copies, dashboards and exports. Restrict access and apply the applicable retention/deletion policy.

    Verify: Verify the old credential is invalid and unauthorized users cannot query affected log records; retain incident evidence in a protected form.

  3. Prevent regressions across the pipeline

    Add secret-safe logging helpers and tests for request headers, nested dictionaries and exception objects; avoid logging entire model/client configurations.

    Verify: Exercise nested credential fields and failures in the deployed collector path and confirm redaction happens before external transmission.

Remaining validation

  • Pattern-based redaction can miss novel secrets or encoded values; minimize collected content and permissions rather than relying on masking alone.

Related controls: DATA-03

Fix guidance sources
  • CISA-SECURE-BY-DESIGN / Shifting the Balance of Cybersecurity Risk: Principles and Approaches for Secure by Design Software
  • JOINT-DEPLOY / Deploying AI Systems Securely: Best Practices for Deploying Secure and Resilient AI Systems

Weakness mappings: CWE-532

Finding references
  • https://www.cisa.gov/resources-tools/resources/secure-by-design
Record your review unreviewed

lowopenAI024

Container image is not digest pinned

dotnet/src/Microsoft.AutoGen/AgentHost/Dockerfile:4–4

Finding ID: 1b457f2ddf5f7bd1f2b8ff83
Confidence: low · Category: supply_chain

A container base or deployment image uses a mutable reference rather than a content digest. The bytes can change between builds or deployments.

Observed evidence

FROM mcr.microsoft.com/dotnet/aspnet:8.0 AS base

Remediation: Pin approved image digests, track provenance and SBOMs, and update through reviewed vulnerability-remediation workflows.

Fix plan and agent/MCP relevance

Pin the approved image content digest and maintain a reviewed update process.

Why this matters for agents/MCP: Agent/MCP deployments may execute a different tool stack when a mutable image tag moves. That changes the code holding credentials and enforcing tool policy without a matching application source change.

Inspect the reported source location and its callers, change the implementation or effective configuration, then rebuild and rescan. The scan does not establish that this code is on an active agent or MCP request path.

Confirm applicability

  • Inspect the image selected by the actual build/deployment and platform; tags may already be constrained by external admission policy.
  • Digest pinning controls content identity, not whether the pinned content is safe or current.
  1. Record and pin the intended digest

    Replace mutable base/deployment references with the approved registry image@sha256 digest, retaining a human-readable version where useful. Record the intended platform or multi-platform index.

    Verify: Resolve the approved reference through the build/deployment system and verify the running image digest matches the recorded artifact/platform.

  2. Review provenance and contents

    Associate the digest with build provenance, an SBOM and a separate vulnerability scan; verify the publisher and build process before approving it.

    Verify: Trace the selected digest to the expected build and verify the SBOM and vulnerability results apply to that exact artifact.

  3. Update pins deliberately

    Use automation to propose digest updates, rebuild and run agent/MCP regression and policy checks, then roll out the reviewed artifact.

    Verify: Exercise rollback to a previously approved digest and confirm updates do not silently widen runtime permissions.

Remaining validation

  • A digest can preserve a vulnerable version indefinitely if updates are neglected; this scanner does not perform a CVE feed lookup.

Related controls: SUP-01, SUP-03

Fix guidance sources
  • REM-DOCKER-BUILD / Docker: pin base image versions
  • JOINT-DEPLOY / Deploying AI Systems Securely: Best Practices for Deploying Secure and Resilient AI Systems

Weakness mappings: CWE-829

Finding references
  • https://www.cisa.gov/resources-tools/resources/secure-by-design
Record your review unreviewed

AI024 Container image is not digest pinned — python/packages/autogen-studio/Dockerfile:1

Understand the evidence

How the review layers work

  1. 01 / Deterministic

    Inspect selected artifacts

    Bounded parsers and static rules collect reproducible file, configuration and image observations. Scope limits and read or parse failures remain visible.

  2. 02 / Optional model

    Review bounded evidence

    An explicitly enabled model can identify contextual concerns. Deterministic selection, budgets, schema checks and exact-quote checks constrain its input and output. Its judgment remains advisory.

  3. 03 / Human and runtime

    Verify deployed controls

    Owners validate access boundaries, approvals, isolation and recovery in the deployed system. Record your evidence and unresolved work in this report.

Scenario a scan can missWhat each review layer can establishWhat still needs evidence
Execution and data flow
Cross-file flows, dynamic imports, wrappers, reflection, generated code, unsupported languages and runtime values can hide a risk. A dangerous API can also be intentional and correctly constrained.		Deterministic: Python AST checks with bounded local aliases/values; JavaScript/TypeScript tokens and balanced calls identify selected eval, shell, deserialization, SQL and template patterns.
Optional model: A model can explain a retrieved call path, question whether input is trusted and identify contextual concerns the pattern rules do not encode.		Exercise actual entry points with authorized negative tests; verify input provenance, execution privileges and sandbox boundaries.
MCP identity, authorization and tools
Configuration may differ from deployment. External gateways, identity providers, token audience checks and tenant boundaries may be absent from retrieved evidence.		Deterministic: Selected configuration/code signals flag insecure transport, explicit authentication bypass, token passthrough, broad tool grants and unpinned server launch packages.
Optional model: A model can review supplied authorization logic, tool descriptions and trust-boundary evidence for missing assumptions or inconsistent checks.		Test expired/wrong-audience tokens, cross-tenant access, per-tool authorization, consent binding and the deployed MCP transport.
Prompt injection, retrieval and memory
Adaptive attacks, multi-turn interactions and tool chains depend on runtime behavior. Retrieval can omit the relevant code; a model can overlook an attack or be influenced by hostile evidence.		Deterministic: Selected patterns identify external content placed in privileged model messages and explicit approval/sandbox bypass. This is not an attack simulation.
Optional model: A model can reason about untrusted-content handling, persistent memory, tool-output influence and missing approval boundaries in the supplied excerpts.		Run isolated adversarial agent/MCP evaluations with attack-success and legitimate-task measurements, plus human review of consequential actions.
Secrets and data handling
Encoded, split or runtime-fetched credentials can be missed; labels/test values can resemble secrets. Neither layer establishes whether a credential is live or redaction is complete.		Deterministic: Selected literal credentials, plausible private-key material, credential-bearing URLs, logging patterns and browser credential exposure are flagged and redacted best-effort.
Optional model: A model can identify sensitive data flows and retention concerns when the supplied evidence explains their context.		Verify secret stores, credential rotation, logging/retention policy, egress destinations and actual data-access permissions.
Built images and supply chain
Compiled logic, live deployment overrides, signature trust and dependency CVEs are not assessed. Package inventory and archive consistency do not establish safety or publisher identity.		Deterministic: Image archives are reconstructed without starting containers; supported packaged source, metadata, retained credentials, stored permissions and package inventories are inspected.
Optional model: A model can interpret supplied image/source evidence, flag missing deployment context and propose verification work.		Use vulnerability/advisory analysis, provenance/signature verification and deployment testing alongside the static image assessment.
Operations and control effectiveness
A valid citation proves that a quote exists, not that the model's interpretation is correct. Policies, external services, incident procedures and production outcomes may be unavailable.		Deterministic: The catalog retains governance, monitoring, recovery, approval and isolation checks even where no deterministic rule maps to them.
Optional model: Full review queues every active acceptance check and can identify evidence gaps or propose runtime/human validation; it can cite exact submitted source text.		Record owner-reviewed evidence, operational exercises and runtime results. A written justification remains an accepted exception, never an automatically validated pass.
Complete scan and review workflow
  1. Choose an explicit code/image target and scan configuration; no target code or container is started.
  2. Collect bounded deterministic evidence, record hashes, rule matches, exclusions and coverage gaps.
  3. When explicitly enabled, select bounded evidence for finding triage and every active control check; findings-only mode narrows this step.
  4. Run advisory model requests under fixed limits; validate response schemas, known identifiers and exact evidence quotes. The model cannot select files or execute tools through the review controller.
  5. Retain unsupported claims and runtime/human requirements; record user review decisions separately from model advice.
  6. Import an explicitly selected reviewed report with a fresh target scan; revalidate evidence bindings, retain stale decisions for audit and recompute the result.

Neither layer provides a numerical security score, complete attack coverage, or compliance certification. Human notes and proposed verification are not proof that a check passed.

Reproduce the selected run

Scan configuration

SettingRecorded valueEffect on this report
Input modesource directoryOnly the selected artifact is inspected; no target application is executed.
Severity gatehighOnly open findings at this threshold or higher affect the finding gate; operational gaps remain separate.
Active static rules42Justified/disabled rule matches remain audit observations and receive no positive or negative count.
Maximum source file bytes20000000A larger selected file creates a visible coverage gap.
Total source read budget300000000The bounded read budget includes conservative charging for failed reads.
Maximum source files / traversal entries20000 / 100000A reached limit leaves work incomplete, rather than silently treating omitted content as safe.
Finding judgedisabledOnly explicitly enabled review sends a bounded payload. The model cannot change recorded static severity.
Control analystdisabledOnly active acceptance checks enter the optional queue.
Control requests used / allowed0 / not enabledThe finding-triage request is a separate request; budgets do not guarantee completed validation.
Configured optional review provider / modeNone / fullConfigured request settings; the actual stage results above show whether the requested work completed.
Configured finding limit / extra source100 / FalseControls finding-triage scope; full-mode source selection has separate evidence budgets.

Scope and assurance

What this scan could establish

Coverage requiring attention

No traversal, parsing, or resource-limit gaps were recorded inside the selected scope.

Excluded directories, unsupported languages, deployment configuration, and runtime behavior may remain outside that scope.

Unknowns and follow-up validation

  • Actual reachability, deployment configuration, upstream validation, data sensitivity, and exploitability require verification.

Model output is nondeterministic and advisory. It cannot dismiss deterministic findings, change their severity, establish compliance, or change the deterministic CI gate.

Disabled. No optional LLM request was made. The deterministic scan and this report run independently.

Complete control checklist

Controls and acceptance checks

Back to top

All 66 catalog controls appear below. Expand a control for acceptance checks, source guidance, mapped findings, and any optional analyst review.

These are project-defined checks mapped to published guidance, not official benchmark scores. no_pattern_detected means only that the mapped detector did not fire. findings_detected requires investigation; it is not an automatic compliance failure.
